\delta Z_{i}^{(1)}
,
\delta Z_{i}^{(2)}
,…, become determined by the renormalization conditions, to be discussed in the sections below.

9.4
Geometric Lagrangian density of UGM in terms of renormalized quantities

Next, we present the Lagrangian density of UGM in terms of the renormalized quantities. First, we recognize that the quantities of the Lagrangian density of UGM in equations (
111
) and (
112
) are bare quantities, even though
this has not been indicated
by any subscripts in these equations. Using the expressions of the bare quantities in terms of the renormalized quantities in equation (
160
) and applying the renormalization factor equalities in equations (
161
)–(
163
), the terms of the Lagrangian density of UGM in equation (
112
) become

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{D,kin}}

\displaystyle=\frac{i\hbar c}{2}\bar{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu}\vec{\partial}_{\nu}-\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\partial$}}$}_{\nu}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu})\psi-m_{\mathrm{e}}c^{2}\bar{\psi}\psi

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+(Z_{\psi}-1)\frac{i\hbar c}{2}\bar{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu}\vec{\partial}_{\nu}-\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\partial$}}$}_{\nu}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu})\psi

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-(Z_{\psi}Z_{m}-1)m_{\mathrm{e}}c^{2}\bar{\psi}\psi,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{em,kin}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\mu_{0}}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}-(Z_{A}-1)\frac{1}{4\mu_{0}}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{em,int}}

\displaystyle=-q_{\mathrm{e}}c\bar{\psi}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu}\psi A_{\nu}-(Z_{\psi}-1)q_{\mathrm{e}}c\bar{\psi}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu}\psi A_{\nu},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{g,kin}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\kappa}H_{\rho\mu\nu}S^{\rho\mu\nu}+(Z_{H}-1)\frac{1}{4\kappa}H_{\rho\mu\nu}S^{\rho\mu\nu},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{g,int}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{g^{\prime}_{\mathrm{g}}}{g_{\mathrm{g}}}\Big\{\frac{c}{2}P^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma}[i\hbar\bar{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F\rho}}\vec{\partial}_{\sigma}-\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\partial$}}$}_{\rho}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F\sigma}})\psi

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-q_{\mathrm{e}}\bar{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F\rho}}A_{\sigma}+A_{\rho}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F\sigma}})\psi-m^{\prime}_{\mathrm{e}}c\eta_{\rho\sigma}\bar{\psi}\psi]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\frac{1}{2\mu_{0}}P^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma,\eta\lambda}\partial_{\rho}A_{\sigma}\partial_{\eta}A_{\lambda}\Big\}H_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\frac{g^{\prime}_{\mathrm{g}}}{g_{\mathrm{g}}}\Big\{\frac{c}{2}P^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma}[(Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}\!-\!1)i\hbar\bar{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F\rho}}\vec{\partial}_{\sigma}\!-\!\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\partial$}}$}_{\rho}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F\sigma}})\psi

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-(Z_{\mathrm{g}\psi}-1)q_{\mathrm{e}}\bar{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F\rho}}A_{\sigma}+A_{\rho}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F\sigma}})\psi

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-(Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}Z_{\mathrm{g}m}-1)m^{\prime}_{\mathrm{e}}c\eta_{\rho\sigma}\bar{\psi}\psi]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+(Z_{\mathrm{g}A}-1)\frac{1}{2\mu_{0}}P^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma,\eta\lambda}\partial_{\rho}A_{\sigma}\partial_{\eta}A_{\lambda}\Big\}H_{\mu\nu},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{em,gf}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\mu_{0}\xi_{\mathrm{e}}}(\partial_{\nu}A^{\nu})^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{g,gf}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa\xi_{\mathrm{g}}}\eta_{\gamma\delta}P^{\alpha\beta,\lambda\gamma}P^{\rho\sigma,\eta\delta}\partial_{\lambda}H_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\eta}H_{\rho\sigma}.

(165)

The terms of the Lagrangian density in equation (
165
) involving the renormalization factors are called the counterterms. When the renormalization factors are set equal to unity, the counterterms become zero and the Lagrangian density terms in equation (
165
) become identical in their form to the terms of equation (
112
). The gauge-fixing Lagrangian densities
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{em,gf}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{g,gf}}
in equation (
165
) do not obtain counterterms due to the renormalization relations of the gauge-fixing parameters, given in equation (
163
). The counterterm Feynman diagrams follow unambiguously from the Lagrangian density in equation (
165
). These counterterm diagrams are presented together with the other Feynman diagrams in table
2
.

9.5
Computational approach

In the study of the renormalization of unified gravity at 1-loop order, we need to calculate several 1-loop Feynman diagrams. The literature on the computation of Feynman loop diagrams is extensive
Weinzierl2022
;
Smirnov2006
;
Harlander2024
. For all loop integrals of unified gravity, the so-called Passarino–Veltman reduction can be applied to transform the more complicated integrals into simpler ones, which can be solved by standard techniques known from previous literature. For both the Passarino–Veltman reduction and the calculation of the simpler loop integrals, there are highly automated tools available. In this work, after writing the loop integrals, we systematically reduce and calculate them using Wolfram Mathematica packages FeynCalc
Shtabovenko2016
and Package-X
Patel2015
.

To regularize the divergences that are obtained in the calculation of Feynman loop diagrams, we follow FeynCalc and Package-X to use dimensional regularization with the space-time dimension set to
D=4-2\epsilon
, where
\epsilon
is a small positive number. In the regularization of UV divergences,
\epsilon
is denoted by
\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}
, and in the regularization of IR divergences,
\epsilon
is denoted by
\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}
. The use of these notations clarifies the origin of the divergences in our results for the renormalization factors in table
3
.

9.6
Renormalization of the electron self-energy

We start the calculations of the renormalization of unified gravity from the electron self-energy. The electron self-energy refers to the quantum corrections to the electron propagator due to its interaction with the electromagnetic field. In QED, the electron self-energy correction arises from virtual photon exchanges between the electron and the vacuum, which leads to a modification of the electron’s mass and wavefunction. In unified gravity, the electron self-energy becomes also contributed by the virtual graviton exchanges.

The 1-photon-loop, 1-graviton-loop, and counterterm contributions to the electron propagator are, respectively, given by the following irreducible Feynman diagrams:

\displaystyle-i\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}=\hbox{\raise 10.81218pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_026}}}}}}}$}}}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{-i\eta_{\mu\nu}}{(p-k)^{2}}\Big(\frac{ie\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}\hbar c}}\Big)\frac{i(\not{k}+m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4})}{k^{2}-m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\frac{ie\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}\hbar c}}\Big)\frac{d^{D}k}{(2\pi)^{D}}

\displaystyle=\frac{i\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big\{\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big](\not{p}-4m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1\!+\!\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)\not{p}\!-\!6m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}\!+\!\Big(1\!-\!\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)\log\!\Big(\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}-p^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\Big[\Big(1+\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)\not{p}-4m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}\Big]\Big\},

(166)

\displaystyle-i\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}=\hbox{\raise 11.9501pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_027}}}}}}}$}}}

\displaystyle=\int\!\frac{iP_{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}^{(D)}}{(p-k)^{2}}\Big\{\!-\!\frac{i}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P_{\;\;\;\;\;\rho\sigma}^{\alpha\beta,}[\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\rho}(p+k)^{\sigma}-\eta^{\rho\sigma}m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}]\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\cdot\frac{i(\not{k}+m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4})}{k^{2}-m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big\{-\frac{i}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P_{\;\;\;\;\;\mu\nu}^{\eta\lambda,}[\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu}(p+k)^{\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\eta^{\mu\nu}m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}]\Big\}\frac{d^{D}k}{(2\pi)^{D}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i\kappa cp^{2}}{64\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big\{\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big](\not{p}-5m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1+\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}+\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}\Big)\not{p}-8m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1\!-\!\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)\log\!\Big(\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}-p^{2}}\Big)\Big[\Big(1\!+\!\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\!+\!\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}\Big)\not{p}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\Big(5+\frac{3m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}\Big]\Big\},

(167)

\displaystyle-i\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_028}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle=i[\delta Z_{\psi}^{(1)}\not{p}-(\delta Z_{\psi}^{(1)}+\delta Z_{\mathrm{m}}^{(1)})m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}].

(168)

Here
\mu
is the arbitrary scale constant of the dimensional regularization, and
\gamma
is the Euler–Mascheroni constant. The red crosses on the external lines highlight that these lines are not included in the expressions. The renormalized electron self-energy 1-loop amplitude is given by the sum of the terms in equations (
166
)–(
168
) as

\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L}}=\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}+\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}+\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}.

(169)

The renormalization conditions for the electron self-energy in the on-shell renormalization scheme are well known to be given by
Schwartz2014
;
Peskin2018

\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L}}\big|_{\not{p}=m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}}=\mathbf{0},\hskip 11.38092pt\frac{d\Sigma_{\mathrm{1L}}}{d\not{p}}\bigg|_{\not{p}=m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}}=\mathbf{0}.

(170)

The zero values of these renormalization conditions mean that, at the physically meaningful on-shell renormalization point, the renormalized electron propagator has the same form as in the free-particle theory in the absence of loop corrections.

Using the renormalization conditions in equation (
170
), the 1-photon-loop and 1-graviton-loop contributions to the renormalization factors
Z_{\mathrm{m}}
and
Z_{\psi}
, in the on-shell renormalization scheme, are determined to be

\delta Z_{\mathrm{m,photon}}^{(1)}=-\frac{3\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{4}{3}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(171)

\delta Z_{\mathrm{\psi,photon}}^{(1)}=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{2}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+4+3\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(172)

\delta Z_{\mathrm{m,graviton}}^{(1)}=\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{16\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+1+\!\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(173)

\delta Z_{\mathrm{\psi,graviton}}^{(1)}=-\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{64\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{7}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{4}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+10+3\!\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(174)

The pole parts of equations (
171
)–(
174
) are proportional to
1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}
and
1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}
and they appear in some form in all renormalization schemes
Schwartz2014
. The physical observables of a renormalized theory are independent of the renormalization scheme, the scale constant, the Euler–Mascheroni constant, and the regularization parameters, such as
\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}
and
\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}
. This independence is shown for selected radiative corrections in section
10
.

9.7
Renormalization of the photon self-energy

Next, we study the photon self-energy. In QED, the photon self-energy correction arises from the creation and annihilation of virtual electron–positron pairs. It is also called the electromagnetic vacuum polarization. In unified gravity, the photon self-energy becomes also contributed by the virtual graviton exchanges.

The 1-electron-loop, 1-graviton-loop, and counterterm contributions to the photon propagator are described by the following irreducible Feynman diagrams:

\displaystyle i\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mu\nu}=\hbox{\raise 0.56905pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_029}}}}}}}$}}}

\displaystyle=-\int\mathrm{Tr}\Big[\frac{i(\not{k}+m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4})}{k^{2}-m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\frac{ie\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}\hbar c}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\cdot\frac{i(\not{p}+\not{k}+m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4})}{(p+k)^{2}-m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\frac{ie\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}\hbar c}}\Big)\Big]\frac{d^{D}k}{(2\pi)^{D}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big\{\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)+\frac{5}{3}+\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1+\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\log\!\Big[1\!+\!\frac{p^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}\!-\!1\Big)\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times(p^{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}-p^{\mu}p^{\nu}),

(175)

\displaystyle i\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mu\nu}=\hbox{\raise 9.38945pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_030}}}}}}}$}}}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{iP_{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}^{(D)}}{(p-k)^{2}}\frac{-i\eta_{\rho\sigma}}{k^{2}}\Big(-i\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P^{\alpha\beta,\nu\xi,\rho\kappa}p_{\xi}k_{\kappa}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\Big(-i\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P^{\eta\lambda,\sigma\zeta,\mu\delta}k_{\zeta}p_{\delta}\Big)\frac{d^{D}k}{(2\pi)^{D}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i\kappa cp^{2}}{24\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{1}{6}+\log\!\Big(-\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{p^{2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times(p^{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}-p^{\mu}p^{\nu}),

(176)

\displaystyle i\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_031}}}}}}}$}=-i\delta Z_{\mathrm{A}}^{(1)}(p^{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}-p^{\mu}p^{\nu}).

(177)

The 1-loop photon propagator correction is equal to
i\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu\nu}
multiplied by the tree-level photon propagator from the left and right. Thus, the corrected photon propagator is given by

\displaystyle\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_032}}}}}}}$}\;\;+\;\;\hbox{\raise-0.56905pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_033}}}}}}}$}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-i\eta_{\mu\nu}}{p^{2}}+\frac{-i\eta_{\mu\mu^{\prime}}}{p^{2}}i\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\frac{-i\eta_{\nu^{\prime}\nu}}{p^{2}}

\displaystyle=(1+\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}})\frac{-i\eta_{\mu\nu}}{p^{2}}+i\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{4}}.

(178)

Here
\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}
is the scalar 1-loop photon self-energy renormalization factor, whose value is given below. In the S-matrix element calculations, at least one end of the renormalized photon propagator in equation (
178
) connects to a fermion line. In the summation over all places along the line where it could connect, one finds, according to the Ward identity, that the terms proportional to
p_{\mu}
or
p_{\nu}
vanish
Peskin2018
. Therefore, in the calculation of the S-matrix elements, the last term in the last line of equation (
178
) can be dropped out.

The scalar 1-loop renormalization factor
\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}
of photon self-energy and its electron-loop, graviton-loop, and counterterm parts following from equations (
175
)–(
178
) are given by

\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}=\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}+\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}+\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT}},

(179)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big\{\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)+\frac{5}{3}+\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1+\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\log\!\Big[1\!+\!\frac{p^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}\!-\!1\Big)\Big]\Big\},

(180)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{24\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{1}{6}+\log\!\Big(-\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{p^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(181)

\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}=\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,electron}}+\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,graviton}},

(182)

\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,electron}}=-\delta Z_{A,\mathrm{electron}}^{(1)},

(183)

\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,graviton}}=-\delta Z_{A,\mathrm{graviton}}^{(1)}.

(184)

In the on-shell renormalization scheme, the renormalization conditions for the photon self-energy are given by

\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}\big|_{p^{2}=0}=0,\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}}{p^{4}}\bigg|_{p^{2}=\infty}=0.

(185)

The first condition in equation (
185
) is the standard on-shell renormalization condition for the photon self-energy in quantum field theory
Schwartz2014
;
Peskin2018
. It means that, at the physically meaningful on-shell renormalization point, the renormalized photon propagator has the same form as in the free-particle theory in the absence of loop corrections. The second condition in equation (
185
) is imposed to guarantee that the loop corrections to the Coulomb potential are integrable functions. Through the renormalization constants, one could easily add to the quantity
\Pi_{\mathrm{1L}}
arbitrary analytic terms proportional to
p^{2}
or its higher powers so that the first condition in equation (
185
) remains satisfied. Therefore, to avoid the addition of arbitrary terms that ruin the integrability of the Coulomb potential corrections studied in section
10.1
, the second condition in equation (
185
) is imposed.

The 1-electron-loop and 1-graviton-loop contributions to the renormalization factor
Z_{\mathrm{A}}
, in the on-shell renormalization scheme, are determined to be

\delta Z_{A,\mathrm{electron}}^{(1)}=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(186)

\delta Z_{A,\mathrm{graviton}}^{(1)}=-\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{24\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{1}{6}+\log(4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma})\Big].

(187)

Using the values of the renormalization factors in equation (
186
) and (
187
), the 1-loop renormalized scalar amplitude factors of the photon self-energy become

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}+\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,electron}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big\{\frac{5}{3}+\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}+\Big(1+\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\log\!\Big[1\!+\!\frac{p^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}\!-\!1\Big)\Big]\Big\},

(188)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}+\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,graviton}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{24\pi^{2}\hbar}\log(-p^{2}).

(189)

In section
10.1
, these renormalized correction factors are applied to calculate the radiative corrections to the Coulomb potential.

9.8
Renormalization of the graviton self-energy

Next, we study the graviton self-energy. The graviton self-energy correction arises from the creation and annihilation of virtual electron–positron pairs. It is also contributed by the virtual photon exchanges between the graviton and the vacuum. In contrast to the previous effective field theory quantization of general relativity
Schwartz2014
;
Bambi2023
;
Casadio2022
;
Donoghue1994a
;
Donoghue1994b
, in unified gravity, the gravitons do not interact directly with each other in the vertex interactions. The consistent renormalization of the 1-electron-loop correction to the graviton self-energy in unified gravity is a substantial breakthrough since the conventional gravity in the presence of matter is well known to be nonrenormalizable starting from the 1-loop order
Hooft1974
;
Deser1974a
;
Deser1974b
;
Deser1974c
.

The 1-electron-loop, 1-photon-loop, and counterterm contributions to the graviton self-energy are given by the following irreducible Feynman diagrams:

\displaystyle i\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_034}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle=-\int\mathrm{Tr}\Big[\frac{i(\not{k}+m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4})}{k^{2}-m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big\{\!-\!\frac{i}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P_{\;\;\;\;\;\kappa\delta}^{\alpha\beta,}[\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\kappa}(p+2k)^{\delta}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\eta^{\kappa\delta}m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}]\Big\}\frac{i(\not{p}+\not{k}+m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4})}{(p+k)^{2}-m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\cdot\Big\{\!-\!\frac{i}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P_{\;\;\;\;\;\rho\sigma}^{\eta\lambda,}[\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\rho}(p+2k)^{\sigma}-\eta^{\rho\sigma}m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}]\Big\}\Big]\frac{d^{D}k}{(2\pi)^{D}}

\displaystyle=\frac{i\kappa cp^{4}}{480\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big\{\hat{P}_{\mathrm{A}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}+\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big]\hat{P}_{\mathrm{B}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\!\Big(1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)^{3/2}\log\!\Big[1\!+\!\frac{p^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}\!-\!1\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\hat{P}_{\mathrm{C}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}\Big\},

(190)

\displaystyle i\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_035}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\frac{-i\eta^{\mu\nu}}{k^{2}}\frac{-i\eta^{\rho\sigma}}{(p+k)^{2}}\Big(-i\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P^{\alpha\beta,\mu\kappa,\rho\xi}k_{\kappa}(p+k)_{\xi}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\Big(-i\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P^{\eta\lambda,\sigma\delta,\nu\zeta}(p+k)_{\delta}k_{\zeta}\Big)\frac{d^{D}k}{(2\pi)^{D}}

\displaystyle=\!\frac{i\kappa cp^{4}}{240\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big\{\!\hat{P}_{81,94,30}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}\!+\!\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}\!+\!\log\!\Big(\!\!-\!\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{p^{2}}\Big)\Big]\!\hat{P}_{3,2,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}\Big\},

(191)

\displaystyle i\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}

\displaystyle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_036}}}}}}}$}=i(Z_{H}-1)p^{2}\hat{P}_{1,2,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}.

(192)

The factor of
\frac{1}{2}
in front of the integral in equation (
191
) comes from the division by the symmetry factor of the diagram according to the standard Feynman rule. The quantities
\hat{P}_{a,b,c}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
,
\hat{P}_{\mathrm{A}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
,
\hat{P}_{\mathrm{B}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
, and
\hat{P}_{\mathrm{C}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
in equations (
190
)–(
192
) are defined below. First, the quantity
P_{a,b,c}^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma}
without a hat is a generalization of
P^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma}
in equation (
51
) for the integer parameters
a,b
and
c
, defined as

P_{a,b,c}^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma}=\frac{1}{2c}(a\eta^{\mu\sigma}\eta^{\rho\nu}+a\eta^{\mu\rho}\eta^{\nu\sigma}-b\eta^{\mu\nu}\eta^{\rho\sigma}).

(193)

Here
\eta^{\alpha\beta}
is the Minkowski metric tensor. Analogously, the quantity
\hat{P}_{a,b,c}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
with a hat is defined as

\hat{P}_{a,b,c}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}=\frac{1}{2c}(a\hat{\eta}^{\alpha\lambda}\hat{\eta}^{\beta\eta}+a\hat{\eta}^{\alpha\eta}\hat{\eta}^{\beta\lambda}-b\hat{\eta}^{\alpha\beta}\hat{\eta}^{\eta\lambda}).

(194)

Here the quantity
\hat{\eta}^{\alpha\beta}
is a projection defined as

\hat{\eta}^{\alpha\beta}=\eta^{\alpha\beta}-\frac{p^{\alpha}p^{\beta}}{p^{2}}.

(195)

The derived quantities
\hat{P}_{\mathrm{A}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
,
\hat{P}_{\mathrm{B}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
, and
\hat{P}_{\mathrm{C}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
in equation (
190
) are defined as

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{\mathrm{A}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}

\displaystyle=4\hat{P}_{27,23,15}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}-\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\hat{P}_{19,86,3}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\frac{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}(64\hat{P}_{1,-1,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}+45P_{1,0,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}),

(196)

\hat{P}_{\mathrm{B}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}=\hat{P}_{3,2,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}-\frac{10m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\hat{P}_{1,2,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}-\frac{30m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}P_{1,0,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda},

(197)

\hat{P}_{\mathrm{C}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}=\hat{P}_{3,2,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}+\frac{8m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\hat{P}_{1,-1,1}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}.

(198)

The 1-graviton-loop propagator correction is equal to
i\Xi_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}
multiplied by the tree-level graviton propagator from the left and right. Thus, the corrected graviton propagator is given by

\displaystyle\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_037}}}}}}}$}\;\;+\;\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_038}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle=\frac{iP_{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}}{p^{2}}+\frac{iP_{\alpha\beta\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}}{p^{2}}i\Xi_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime}}\frac{iP_{\eta^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime}\eta\lambda}}{p^{2}}

\displaystyle=(1+\Xi_{\mathrm{1L}})\frac{iP_{\alpha\beta,\eta\lambda}}{p^{2}}.

(199)

Here the renormalized scalar factor
\Xi_{\mathrm{1L}}
is formed from the 1-photon-loop, 1-electron-loop, and counterterm contributions as

\Xi_{\mathrm{1L}}=\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}+\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}+\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT}},

(200)

\displaystyle\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{32\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big\{\frac{37}{15}-\frac{20m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{3p^{2}}-\frac{40m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}-\frac{16m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}\Big)\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1-\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}-\frac{8m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}\Big)\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\log\Big[1+\frac{p^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}-1\Big)\Big]\Big\},

(201)

\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}=-\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{16\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{29}{30}+\log\!\Big(-\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{p^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(202)

\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}=\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,electron}}+\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,photon}},

(203)

\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,electron}}=-6\delta Z_{\mathrm{H,electron}}^{(1)},

(204)

\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,photon}}=-6\delta Z_{\mathrm{H,photon}}^{(1)}.

(205)

In the on-shell renormalization scheme, the renormalization conditions for the graviton self-energy in unified gravity are analogous to the renormalization conditions for the photon self-energy in section
9.7
. The graviton self-energy renormalization conditions are given by

\Xi_{\mathrm{1L}}\big|_{p^{2}=0}=0,\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\Xi_{\mathrm{1L}}}{p^{4}}\bigg|_{p^{2}=\infty}=0.

(206)

In analogy with the photon self-energy renormalization conditions in equation (
185
), the second condition in equation (
206
) is imposed to guarantee that the loop corrections to the Newtonian potential are integrable functions. Through the renormalization factors, one could easily add to the quantity
\Xi_{\mathrm{1L}}
arbitrary analytic terms proportional to
p^{2}
or its higher powers so that the first condition in equation (
206
) remains satisfied. Therefore, to avoid the addition of arbitrary terms that ruin the integrability of the Newtonian potential corrections studied in section
10.2
, the second condition in equation (
206
) is imposed.

The renormalization conditions of the graviton self-energy in equation (
206
) lead to the 1-photon-loop and 1-electron-loop counterterms for the graviton self-energy, with the 1-loop contributions to the renormalization factors given by

\displaystyle\delta Z_{\mathrm{H,electron}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{192\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big\{\frac{37}{15}-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}-\frac{24m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}+\log(m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}-\frac{16m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{4}}\Big)\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big]\Big\},

(207)

\delta Z_{\mathrm{H,photon}}^{(1)}=-\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{96\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{29}{30}+\log(4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma})\Big].

(208)

The renormalized 1-photon and 1-electron loop correction factors to the graviton propagator are given by

\displaystyle\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}+\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,electron}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{32\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big\{-\frac{8m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{3p^{2}}\Big(1+\frac{6m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}\Big)-\log(m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\Big(1-\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}-\frac{8m_{\mathrm{e}}^{4}c^{4}}{p^{4}}\Big)\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\log\Big[1+\frac{p^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p^{2}}}-1\Big)\Big]\Big\},

(209)

\displaystyle\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}

\displaystyle=\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}+\Xi_{\mathrm{1L,CT,photon}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{16\pi^{2}\hbar}\log(-p^{2}).

(210)

In section
10.2
, these renormalized correction factors are used to calculate radiative corrections to the Newtonian potential.

9.9
Photon–graviton two-point function

Next, we study the photon–graviton two-point function, which describes possible transformation between photons and gravitons. At the tree level, such a process is nonexistent. At the 1-loop order, the photon–graviton two-point function is formed by the Feynman diagrams with a single electron loop. The explicit calculations of such loop diagrams, however, give zero as

\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_039}}}}}}}$}=0,

(211)

\hbox{\raise 17.35619pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_040}}}}}}}$}}}=0.

(212)

Therefore, the 1-electron-loop contribution to the photon–graviton two-point function vanishes, and we do not need to account for such a process in the renormalization of the vertices, discussed in the sections below.

9.10
Renormalization of the electron–photon vertex

The renormalization of the electron–photon vertex is well known to be associated with the Ward–Takahashi identity
Schwartz2014
;
Peskin2018
. In writing the Lagrangian density in terms of the renormalized quantities in section
9.4
, we used the renormalization factor
Z_{\psi}
both in the counterterm of the electron propagator and in the electron–photon vertex. This is in agreement with equation (
162
). Therefore, in the renormalization of the electron–photon vertex in this section, we do not obtain any new relations for the renormalization factors. Instead, we use the renormalization factors obtained in previous sections to verify that the renormalization condition of the electron–photon vertex is satisfied identically.

The photon index is assumed to be
\mu
but, for compactness, we do not show it in the diagrams below. Accordingly, the 1-photon-loop, 1-graviton-loop, and counterterm contributions to the electron–photon vertex are given by

\displaystyle\frac{-iq_{\mathrm{e}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{\mu}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}\hbar c}}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_041}}}}}}}$},

(213)

\displaystyle\frac{-iq_{\mathrm{e}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}\hbar c}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_042}}}}}}}$}\;+\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_043}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_044}}}}}}}$}\;+\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_045}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_046}}}}}}}$}\;+\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_047}}}}}}}$},

(214)

\displaystyle\frac{-iq_{\mathrm{e}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mathrm{\mu}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}\hbar c}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_048}}}}}}}$}=\delta Z_{\psi}^{(1)}\frac{-iq_{\mathrm{e}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{0}\hbar c}}.

(215)

Apart from the counterterm, we only give the Feynman diagrams since there are many diagrams and the integral expressions are straightforward to write using the Feynman rules presented in table
2
.

The total 1-loop contribution to the electron–photon vertex is given by

\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu}=\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}^{\mu}+\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu}}+\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mu}.

(216)

The counterterm can also be split into photon and graviton parts
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT,photon}}^{\mu}
and
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mu}
based on substituting the renormalization factors of equations (
172
) and (
174
) into equation (
215
).

The on-shell renormalization condition of the electron–photon vertex is imposed by requiring that
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu}
and any higher-order-loop terms do not lead to corrections of the tree-level vertex for
p=p^{\prime}

Schwartz2014
;
Peskin2018
. For on-shell electrons, we additionally have
p^{2}=m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}
. Therefore, the matrix element of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu}
must be zero for
p=p^{\prime}
as

\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu}u(p)\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}=0.

(217)

The vanishing of the quantum corrections of the electron–photon vertex for
p=p^{\prime}
effectively means that the classical form of the Coulomb law applies at asymptotically long distances
Schwartz2014
.

The general structure of the 1-loop radiative correction to the electron–photon vertex matrix element is well known to be of the Lorentz-invariant form, given by
Schwartz2014

\displaystyle\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu}u(p)

\displaystyle=\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\Big[F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu}+F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})\frac{iq_{\nu}}{\hbar m_{\mathrm{e}}c}\hat{S}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu\nu}\Big]u(p).

(218)

Here
q=p^{\prime}-p
is the photon four-momentum,
\hat{S}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu\nu}=\frac{i\hbar}{4}[\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu},\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu}]
is the spin operator for fermions
Partanen2024a
, and the quantities
F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})
and
F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})
are the 1-loop radiative corrections of the total electron–photon-vertex form factors
F_{1}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})
and
F_{2}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})
, which are generally written as series over all loop orders as

F_{1}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})=F_{\mathrm{1,0L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}+F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})+\ldots,

(219)

F_{2}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})=F_{\mathrm{2,0L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}+F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})+\ldots\;.

(220)

Here the leading order terms,
F_{\mathrm{1,0L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}=1
and
F_{\mathrm{2,0L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}=0
, correspond to the tree-level diagram.

Since the renormalization condition in equation (
217
) is determined at
p=p^{\prime}
, we have
q=p^{\prime}-p=0
, and thus, the second term of equation (
218
) is zero. Therefore, the renormalization condition considers the form factor
F_{1}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})
stating that
F_{1}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})
is determined to be
F_{1}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})=1
for
q^{2}=0
at all loop orders. Consequently,
F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})=0
. The second form factor
F_{2}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})
is associated with the anomalous magnetic moment of the electron, discussed in section
10.3
. Since the 1-photon-loop and 1-graviton-loop radiative corrections and the associated counterterm parts are proportional to different physical constants, the quantity
F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}(q^{2})
can be split into the 1-photon-loop and 1-graviton-loop associated parts, which are separately zero for
q^{2}=0
as

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}=F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}+F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}=0,

(221)

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}=F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}+F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}=0.

(222)

The explicit calculation of the 1-photon-loop, 1-graviton-loop, and counterterm contributions to the form factors following from the Feynman diagrams in equations (
213
)–(
215
) show that equations (
221
) and (
222
) are satisfied identically. The 1-photon-loop contribution to equation (
221
) following from equation (
213
) and the associated counterterm contribution part following from equations (
172
) and (
215
) are given for
q^{2}=0
by

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}=\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{2}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+4+3\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(223)

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{2}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+4+3\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(224)

The 1-graviton-loop contributions to equation (
222
) following from each Feynman diagram in equation (
214
) and the associated counterterm contribution part following from equations (
174
) and (
215
) are given for
q^{2}=0
by

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma,diag1)}}=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{g}}}{8\pi}\Big[\frac{7}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{4}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+18+11\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(225)

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma,diag2)}}=F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma,diag3)}}=F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma,diag6)}}=0,

(226)

\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma,diag4)}}=F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma,diag5)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{7\alpha_{\mathrm{g}}}{8\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+2+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(227)

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(e\gamma)}}\!=\!-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{g}}}{8\pi}\!\Big[\frac{7}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{4}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+10+3\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\!\Big].

(228)

The exact cancellation of the terms in equations (
223
) and (
224
) when substituted into equation (
221
) is a consequence of the electromagnetic gauge invariance via the Ward–Takahashi identity
Schwartz2014
;
Peskin2018
. The exact cancellation of the different nonzero terms in equations (
225
), (
227
), and (
228
) when substituted into equation (
222
) is likewise a result of the electromagnetic gauge symmetry, and it shows that the Ward–Takahashi identity of QED is satisfied in unified gravity at 1-loop order. This is considered to be a necessary requirement for the consistency of the theory and it should extend to all loop orders.

9.11
Renormalization of the electron–graviton vertex

Next, we study the renormalization of the electron–graviton vertex, which is related to the renormalization factors
Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}
and
Z_{\mathrm{g}m}
in the Lagrangian density terms of equation (
165
). Through the on-shell renormalization conditions, we obtain unambiguous values for the 1-loop contributions to these renormalization factors.

The graviton indices are assumed to be
\mu
and
\nu
but, for compactness, we do not show them in the diagrams below. Accordingly, the 1-photon-loop, 1-graviton-loop, and counterterm contributions to the electron–graviton vertex are given by the following Feynman diagrams:

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_049}}}}}}}$}\;+\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_050}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle\;+\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_051}}}}}}}$}\;+\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_052}}}}}}}$},

(229)

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu}}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_053}}}}}}}$},

(230)

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu}}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_054}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{i}{2}\sqrt{\dfrac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma}[\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}^{(1)}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}\rho}(p+p^{\prime})_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-(\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}^{(1)}+\delta Z_{\mathrm{g}m}^{(1)})m_{\mathrm{e}}c\eta_{\rho\sigma}\mathbf{I}_{4}]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{i}{4}\sqrt{\dfrac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}\{\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}^{(1)}\eta^{\mu\nu}(\not{p}+\not{p}^{\prime})-2(\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}^{(1)}+\delta Z_{\mathrm{g}m}^{(1)})\eta^{\mu\nu}m_{\mathrm{e}}c\mathbf{I}_{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}^{(1)}[\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu}(p+p^{\prime})^{\nu}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu}(p+p^{\prime})^{\mu}]\}.

(231)

The total 1-loop contribution to the electron–graviton vertex is given by

\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu\nu}=\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu}}+\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu}}+\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu}}.

(232)

The counterterm can also be split into photon and graviton parts
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu}}
based on the different contributions to the renormalization factors obtained below.

In analogy with the on-shell renormalization condition of the electron–photon vertex in equation (
217
), we define the on-shell renormalization condition for the electron–graviton vertex as

\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\Gamma_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mu\nu}u(p)\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}=0.

(233)

The vanishing of the quantum corrections of the electron–graviton vertex for
p=p^{\prime}
effectively means that the classical form of the Newtonian potential applies to the strength of the gravitational interaction at asymptotically long distances.

The 1-loop radiative correction to the electron–graviton vertex matrix element can be written in a Lorentz-invariant form as

\displaystyle\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\Gamma_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mu\nu}u(p)

\displaystyle=\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\Big\{F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}m_{\mathrm{e}}c\eta^{\mu\nu}\mathbf{I}_{4}\!+\!F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}\frac{(p\!+\!p^{\prime})^{\mu}(p\!+\!p^{\prime})^{\nu}}{2m_{\mathrm{e}}c}\mathbf{I}_{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\!F_{\mathrm{3,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}\frac{iq_{\rho}}{\hbar m_{\mathrm{e}}c}[(p+p^{\prime})^{\mu}\hat{S}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\nu\rho}\!+\!(p+p^{\prime})^{\nu}\hat{S}_{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu\rho}]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\!F_{\mathrm{4,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}q^{\mu}q^{\nu}\mathbf{I}_{4}\Big\}u(p).

(234)

Here
F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
,
F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
,
F_{\mathrm{3,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
, and
F_{\mathrm{4,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
are the 1-loop radiative corrections to the electron-graviton vertex form factors. Through the Gordon identity
Peskin2018
, there is some freedom in the representation of the terms of equation (
234
).

At
q=p^{\prime}-p=0
, the last terms of equation (
234
), associated with
F_{\mathrm{3,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
and
F_{\mathrm{4,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
, are zero. Therefore, the renormalization condition in equation (
233
) implies that
F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
and
F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
must be zero at the renormalization point. Since the 1-photon-loop and 1-graviton-loop radiative corrections and the associated counterterm parts are proportional to different physical constants, the quantities
F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
and
F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
can also be split into the 1-photon-loop and 1-graviton-loop associated parts, which are separately zero for
q^{2}=0
as

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}+F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=0,

(235)

F_{\mathrm{2,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=F_{\mathrm{2,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}+F_{\mathrm{2,1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=0,

(236)

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}+F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=0,

(237)

F_{\mathrm{2,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=F_{\mathrm{2,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}+F_{\mathrm{2,1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=0.

(238)

The 1-photon-loop contributions to the form factor
F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
following from each diagram in equation (
229
) when compared with the structure of the vertex matrix element in equation (
234
) are given by

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg,diag1)}}=\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{12\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{22}{3}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(239)

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg,diag2)}}=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{1}{3}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(240)

\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg,diag3)}}=F_{\mathrm{1,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg,diag4)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{2\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{3}{2}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(241)

The corresponding contributions to the form factor
F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
, following from each diagram in equation (
229
) and the structure of the vertex matrix element in equation (
234
), are given by

F_{\mathrm{2,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg,diag1)}}=\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{12\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{6}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+\frac{56}{3}+7\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(242)

F_{\mathrm{2,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg,diag2)}}=\frac{2\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{17}{12}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(243)

\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{2,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg,diag3)}}=F_{\mathrm{2,1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg,diag4)}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+3+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(244)

Based on equations (
239
)–(
244
) and the form-factor renormalization conditions in equations (
235
) and (
236
), the form-factor counterterm parts
F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
and
F_{\mathrm{2,1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
are determined to be

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=-\frac{3\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{4}{3}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(245)

F_{\mathrm{2,1L,CT,photon}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{2}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+4+3\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(246)

Using the counterterm in equation (
231
) and the electron–graviton vertex matrix element in equation (
234
), we then obtain the 1-photon-loop contributions to the renormalization factors
Z_{\mathrm{g}m}
and
Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}
as

\delta Z_{\mathrm{g}m,\mathrm{photon}}^{(1)}=-\frac{3\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{4}{3}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(247)

\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi,photon}}^{(1)}=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{4\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{2}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+4+3\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(248)

The comparison of these equations with the 1-photon-loop contributions to the renormalization factors
Z_{m}
and
Z_{\psi}
in equations (
171
) and (
172
), we observe that the contributions are identical as
\delta Z_{\mathrm{g}m,\mathrm{photon}}^{(1)}=\delta Z_{m,\mathrm{photon}}^{(1)}
and
\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi,photon}}^{(1)}=\delta Z_{\mathrm{\psi,photon}}^{(1)}
. This means that electromagnetic interaction makes no difference between the inertial and gravitational masses. Below, we show that a similar equivalence is not satisfied for 1-graviton-loop contributions.

The 1-graviton-loop contribution to the form factor
F_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
following from the Feynman diagram in equation (
230
) when compared with the structure of the vertex matrix element in equation (
234
) is given by

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=-\frac{5\kappa cp^{2}}{192\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{31}{30}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(249)

The corresponding contribution to the form factor
F_{\mathrm{2,1L}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
following from equation (
230
) and the structure of the vertex matrix element in equation (
234
) is given by

\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{2,1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3\kappa cp^{2}}{576\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{11}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{12}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+\frac{172}{3}+23\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(250)

Based on the renormalization conditions in equations (
237
) and (
238
), the 1-graviton-loop form-factor contributions
F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
and
F_{\mathrm{2,1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}
are given by

F_{\mathrm{1,1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}=\frac{5\kappa cp^{2}}{192\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{31}{30}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(251)

\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{2,1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(eg)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{3\kappa cp^{2}}{576\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{11}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{12}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+\frac{172}{3}+23\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(252)

Using the counterterm in equation (
231
) and the electron–graviton vertex matrix element in equation (
234
), we then obtain the 1-graviton-loop contributions to the renormalization factors
Z_{\mathrm{g}m}
and
Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}
as

\delta Z_{\mathrm{g}m,\mathrm{graviton}}^{(1)}=\frac{5\kappa cp^{2}}{192\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}-\frac{31}{30}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(253)

\displaystyle\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi,graviton}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{192\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{11}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{12}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{IR}}}+\frac{172}{3}+23\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big].

(254)

In contrast to the case of the 1-photon-loop contributions in equations (
247
) and (
248
), the 1-graviton-loop contributions to
Z_{\mathrm{g}m}
and
Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}
in equations (
253
) and (
254
) are not equivalent to the 1-graviton-loop contributions to the renormalization factors
Z_{m}
and
Z_{\psi}
in equations (
173
) and (
174
). This shows that the different renormalization factors of the inertial and gravitational masses are necessary for the renormalizability of unified gravity.

9.12
Renormalization of the photon–graviton vertex

Next, we study the renormalization of the photon–graviton vertex. In unified gravity, the renormalization of this vertex is related to the renormalization factor
Z_{\mathrm{g}A}
in the Lagrangian density terms of equation (
165
). The 1-loop contribution to the renormalization factor
Z_{\mathrm{g}A}
can already be determined based on the equality
Z_{\mathrm{g}A}=Z_{A}Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}/Z_{\psi}
, following from equation (
161
), and the 1-loop contributions to the renormalization factors
Z_{A}
,
Z_{\mathrm{g\psi}}
, and
Z_{\psi}
, determined in the previous sections. Using equations (
172
), (
174
), (
186
), (
187
), (
248
), and (
254
) we then obtain

\displaystyle\delta Z_{\mathrm{g}A,\mathrm{electron}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\delta Z_{A,\mathrm{electron}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big],

(255)

\displaystyle\delta Z_{\mathrm{g}A,\mathrm{graviton}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\delta Z_{A,\mathrm{graviton}}^{(1)}+\delta Z_{\mathrm{g\psi,graviton}}^{(1)}-\delta Z_{\mathrm{\psi,graviton}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa cp^{2}}{8\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\frac{43}{12}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{(m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2})^{4/3}}\Big)\Big].

(256)

Therefore, in the renormalization of the photon–graviton vertex below, we do not obtain any new relations for the renormalization factors but only verify that the renormalization condition of the photon–graviton vertex is satisfied identically.

We assume the same indices for the fields as in the photon–graviton vertex in table
2
but, for compactness, do not show them in the diagrams below. Accordingly, the 1-electron-loop, 1-graviton-loop, and counterterm contributions to the photon–graviton vertex are given by

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\raise 1.13809pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_055}}}}}}}$}}}\;+\;\hbox{\raise 0.85358pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_056}}}}}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\;+\;\hbox{\raise 2.27621pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_057}}}}}}}$}}}\;+\;\hbox{\raise-2.27621pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_058}}}}}}}$}}},

(257)

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}=\hbox{\raise 0.85358pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_059}}}}}}}$}}},

(258)

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{\frac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}=\hbox{\raise 0.85358pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_060}}}}}}}$}}}

\displaystyle=-i\delta Z_{\mathrm{g}A}^{(1)}\sqrt{\dfrac{\kappa c}{\hbar}}P^{\mu\nu,\sigma\eta,\rho\lambda}p^{\prime}_{\eta}p_{\lambda}.

(259)

The total 1-loop contribution to the photon–graviton vertex is given by

\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}=\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}+\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}+\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}.

(260)

The counterterm can also be split into the electron and graviton parts
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT,electron}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}
based on substituting the renormalization factors of equations (
255
) and (
256
) into equation (
259
).

The on-shell renormalization condition of the photon–graviton vertex is imposed by requiring that
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}
and any higher-order-loop terms do not lead to corrections of the tree-level vertex for
p=p^{\prime}
. This requirement is analogous to the renormalization conditions of the electron–photon and electron–graviton vertices above. For on-shell photons, we naturally have
p^{2}=0
. Therefore, the matrix element of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}
must be zero for on-shell states with
p=p^{\prime}
as

\epsilon_{\sigma}^{*}(p^{\prime})\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}\epsilon_{\rho}(p)\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}=0.

(261)

The vanishing of the quantum corrections of the photon–graviton vertex for
p=p^{\prime}
effectively means that the photon–graviton interactions do not modify the classical forms of the Coulomb and Newtonian potentials, which apply to the strengths of the electromagnetic and gravitational interactions at asymptotically long distances.

The scalar product of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}
with the photon polarization vectors in equation (
261
) effectively means that the terms of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}
proportional to
p^{\prime\sigma}p^{\rho}
do not contribute. This is due to the transversality of the photon momentum and polarization vectors, i.e.,
p^{\rho}\epsilon_{\rho}(p)=0
. In more complex calculations of the S-matrix elements, the photon polarization vectors can be replaced by the photon propagators, which connect to fermion lines. In this case, through the summation over all places along the fermion line where the photon propagator could connect, one obtains that the terms proportional to
p^{\rho}
or
p^{\prime\sigma}
vanish. This is due to the Ward identity and is analogous to the discussion on the last term of the photon propagator below equation (
178
).

The 1-electron-loop contributions to the photon–graviton vertex resulting from each Feynman diagram in equation (
257
) are given for
p=p^{\prime}
by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(diag1)\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}=\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(diag2)\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{12\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big](4p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\eta^{\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt-p^{\mu}p^{\rho}\eta^{\nu\sigma}-p^{\nu}p^{\rho}\eta^{\mu\sigma}-p^{\mu}p^{\sigma}\eta^{\nu\rho}-p^{\nu}p^{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\rho})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{15\pi m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}p^{\rho}p^{\sigma},

(262)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(diag3)\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{6\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times p^{\rho}(p^{\mu}\eta^{\nu\sigma}+p^{\nu}\eta^{\mu\sigma}-p^{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\nu}),

(263)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(diag4)\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}}\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{6\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times p^{\sigma}(p^{\mu}\eta^{\nu\rho}+p^{\nu}\eta^{\mu\rho}-p^{\rho}\eta^{\mu\nu}).

(264)

The summation of the contributions of all diagrams, given in equations (
262
)–(
264
), results in the total 1-electron-loop contribution to the photon–graviton vertex without the counterterm as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}+\log\!\Big(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big)\Big]P^{\mu\nu,\sigma\eta,\rho\lambda}p^{\prime}_{\eta}p_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt+\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{15\pi m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}p^{\rho}p^{\sigma}.

(265)

The coefficient of the first term of equation (
265
) is found to be equal in magnitude and opposite in sign to the 1-electron-loop contribution to the renormalization factor
Z_{\mathrm{g}A}
, given in equation (
255
). Therefore, this term becomes cancelled by the electron-loop part of the counterterm in equation (
259
). The contribution of the second term of equation (
265
) vanishes in the S-matrix element calculations according to the discussion below equation (
261
). Therefore, this term can be dropped out, and we conclude that the renormalization condition in equation (
261
) is satisfied.

Correspondingly, the 1-graviton-loop contribution to the photon–graviton vertex resulting from the Feynman diagram in equation (
258
) is given for
p=p^{\prime}
by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mu\nu,\sigma,\rho}\big|_{p=p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\kappa c}{8\pi^{2}\hbar}\Big[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{UV}}}\!+\!\frac{11}{6}\!+\!\log\!\Big(\!-\!\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}e^{-\gamma}}{p^{2}}\Big)\Big]p^{\mu}p^{\nu}p^{\rho}p^{\sigma}.

(266)

Here we have used the on-shell condition of photons,
p^{2}=0
, apart from the logarithm, where
p^{2}
appears in the denominator. By the same argument as discussed in the case of the electron-loop contribution below equations (
233
) and (
265
), the terms proportional to
p^{\rho}
or
p^{\prime\sigma}
do not contribute to the S-matrix elements. Therefore, we conclude that the renormalization condition in equation (
261
) is satisfied, and the 1-graviton loop does not contribute to the renormalization of the photon–graviton vertex. The counterterm in equation (
259
) is in agreement with this result since the 1-graviton-loop contribution to
Z_{\mathrm{g}A}
is zero at the physically meaningful on-shell renormalization point as seen from equation (
256
) with
p^{2}=0
.

9.13
Renormalization of the electron–photon–graviton vertex

Finally, we study the renormalization of the electron–photon–graviton vertex. This vertex is related to the renormalization factor
Z_{\mathrm{g}\psi}
in the Lagrangian density terms of equation (
165
). Since the 1-loop contributions to this renormalization factor is already determined in equations (
248
) and (
254
), in this section we do not obtain any new relations for the renormalization factors but only verify that the renormalization condition of the electron–photon–graviton vertex is satisfied identically.

We assume the same indices for the fields as in the electron–photon–graviton vertex in table
2
but, for compactness, do not show them in the diagrams below. Accordingly, the 1-photon-loop, 1-graviton-loop, and counterterm contributions to the electron–photon–graviton vertex are given by the following Feynman diagrams:

\displaystyle\dfrac{iq_{\mathrm{e}}}{\hbar}\sqrt{\dfrac{\kappa}{\varepsilon_{0}}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\rho}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_061}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_062}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_063}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_064}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_065}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_066}}}}}}}$},

(267)

\displaystyle\dfrac{iq_{\mathrm{e}}}{\hbar}\sqrt{\dfrac{\kappa}{\varepsilon_{0}}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\rho}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_067}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_068}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_069}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_070}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_071}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_072}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle\;+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_073}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_074}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_075}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_076}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_077}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_091}}}}}}}$},

(268)

\displaystyle\dfrac{iq_{\mathrm{e}}}{\hbar}\sqrt{\dfrac{\kappa}{\varepsilon_{0}}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,2P\&3P}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\rho}}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_078}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_079}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_080}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_081}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_082}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle\;+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_083}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_084}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_085}}}}}}}$}+\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_086}}}}}}}$},

(269)

\displaystyle\dfrac{iq_{\mathrm{e}}}{\hbar}\sqrt{\dfrac{\kappa}{\varepsilon_{0}}}\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\rho}}=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[]{feynman_087}}}}}}}$}

\displaystyle=(Z_{\mathrm{g}\psi}-1)\dfrac{iq_{\mathrm{e}}}{\hbar}\sqrt{\dfrac{\kappa}{\varepsilon_{0}}}P^{\mu\nu,\rho\sigma}\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathrm{F}\sigma}.

(270)

Equation (
269
) represents the Feynman diagrams, which are already renormalized by the counterterms of the two-point and three-point functions discussed in the previous sections. Accordingly, each 1L-blob in the Feynman diagrams of equation (
269
) contains the 1-loop diagrams and counterterms associated with the pertinent propagator or vertex.

The total 1-loop contribution to the electron–photon–graviton vertex corresponding to the Feynman diagrams in equations (
267
), (
268
) and (
270
) is given by

\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L}}^{\mu}=\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,photon}}^{\mu\nu,\rho}+\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{\mu\nu,\rho}}+\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT}}^{\mu\nu,\rho}.

(271)

The counterterm can also be split into photon and graviton parts
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT,photon}}^{\mu\nu,\rho}
and
\Gamma_{\mathrm{1L,CT,graviton}}^{\mu\nu,\rho}
based on the different contributions to the renormalization factors obtained below.

In analogy with the on-shell renormalization conditions of the electron–photon vertex in equation (
217
), the electron–graviton vertex in equation (
233
), and the photon–graviton vertex in equation (
261
), we define the on-shell renormalization condition for the electron–photon–graviton vertex. Since it is a four-point vertex, following previous literature
Schwartz2014
, the renormalization condition is defined in terms of the Mandelstam variables. Accordingly, we write the renormalization condition as

\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\Gamma_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mu\nu,\rho}u(p)\epsilon_{\mu\nu}(q^{\prime})\epsilon_{\rho}(q)\Big|_{\begin{array}[]{l}\scriptstyle s=u=m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}\\[-6.0pt]
\scriptstyle t=0\end{array}}=0.

(272)

Here the photon and graviton four-momenta
q
and
q^{\prime}
satisfy
q^{\prime}-q=p^{\prime}-p
.
For the Mandelstam variables, we use the values
s=u=m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}
and
t=0
.

The Mandelstam variable
t=0
implies
p=p^{\prime}
and
q=q^{\prime}
. We consider the rest frame of the electron where
p=p^{\prime}=(m_{\mathrm{e}}c,0,0,0)
. The Mandelstam variables
s=u=m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}
then imply
q=q^{\prime}=(0,0,0,0)
. Through the use of the Gordon identity, all terms of
\bar{u}(p^{\prime})\Gamma_{\mathrm{1,1L}}^{\mu\nu,\rho}u(p)
resulting from the 1-photon and 1-graviton loop diagrams in equations (
267
) and (
268
) and from the counterterm in equation (
270
) are found to be either zero or proportional to
p^{\mu}
,
p^{\nu}
, or
p^{\rho}
at the renormalization point. In the rest frame of the electron, the only nonzero component of the electron four-momentum is the time component. In contrast, since photons and gravitons are massless, the photon polarization four-vector
\epsilon_{\rho}(0)
and the graviton polarization tensor
\epsilon_{\mu\nu}(0)
are defined to have only spatial components. Therefore, the scalar product of the electron four-momentum and the photon polarization four-vector is zero independently of the spatial orientation of the photon polarization four-vector as
p^{\rho}\epsilon_{\rho}(0)=0
. The same applies to the contraction of the electron four-momentum with either of the two indices of the graviton polarization tensor as
p^{\mu}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}(0)=0
and
p^{\nu}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}(0)=0
. Thus, the 1-photon and 1-graviton loop diagrams in equations (
267
) and (
268
) do not contribute to the S-matrix element calculations, and we conclude that the renormalization condition in equation (
272
) is satisfied.

10
Radiative corrections

In this section, we study the radiative corrections of unified gravity in the UGM formulation. In quantum field theories, radiative corrections refer to the modifications in particle interactions and the related physical quantities due to the effects of virtual particles
Schwartz2014
;
Peskin2018
. The virtual-particle interactions are described by the Feynman diagrams as discussed in the previous sections. The radiative corrections follow from the loop-order Feynman diagrams. The radiative corrections play a crucial role in obtaining accurate quantum-field-theoretical predictions for scattering processes, which can be verified experimentally. For example, the prediction of the Standard Model for the electron magnetic moment has been experimentally verified to an astonishing accuracy of one part in a trillion
Fan2023
. In the sections below, we consider how unified gravity leads to the radiative corrections to the Coulomb and Newtonian potentials and to the electron magnetic moment.

10.1
Radiative corrections to the Coulomb potential

As an example of the radiative corrections to the Coulomb potential, we calculate the 1-electron-loop and 1-graviton-loop corrections resulting from the vacuum polarization diagrams and their counterterm in equations (
175
)–(
177
). Detailed study of the contributions resulting from the box and crossed box diagrams, the triangular diagrams, the circular diagram, and the vertex corrections, similar to those studied in
Butt2006
, is left to a separate work.

In the nonrelativistic limit, for the four-momentum of the exchanged photon, we have
p^{2}\rightarrow-|\mathbf{p}|^{2}
, where
\mathbf{p}
is the three-dimensional momentum vector. Thus, the 1-electron-loop and 1-graviton-loop renormalized scalar amplitude factors of the photon self-energy in equations (
188
) and (
189
) become

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(p^{2})\approx\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-|\mathbf{p}|^{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3\pi}\Big\{\frac{5}{3}-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}|^{2}}+\Big(1-\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}|^{2}}\Big)\sqrt{1\!+\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}|^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times\log\!\Big[1\!-\!\frac{|\mathbf{p}|^{2}}{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}\Big(\sqrt{1\!+\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}|^{2}}}\!-\!1\Big)\Big]\Big\},

(273)

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(p^{2})

\displaystyle\approx\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-|\mathbf{p}|^{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\kappa c|\mathbf{p}|^{2}}{24\pi^{2}\hbar}\log(|\mathbf{p}|^{2}).

(274)

The Fourier transform of the Coulomb potential obtains correction terms, which are additions to the tree-level Fourier transform of the Coulomb potential in equation (S27) of the supplementary material. Using equations (
273
) and (
274
), the renormalized 1-electron-loop and 1-graviton-loop correction terms are given by

\widetilde{V}_{\mathrm{e,1L,electron}}(\mathbf{p})=\frac{\hbar^{2}e^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}(|\mathbf{p}|^{2}-i\epsilon)}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-|\mathbf{p}|^{2}),

(275)

\widetilde{V}_{\mathrm{e,1L,graviton}}(\mathbf{p})=\frac{\hbar^{2}e^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}(|\mathbf{p}|^{2}-i\epsilon)}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,graviton}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-|\mathbf{p}|^{2}).

(276)

10.1.1
Renormalized 1-electron-loop contribution

First, we consider the renormalized 1-electron-loop contribution to the radiative correction of the Coulomb potential. This radiative correction is known from QED
Peskin2018
;
Landau1982
and is presented here for completeness and as a background for the 1-graviton-loop contribution calculated below and for the radiative correction of the Newtonian potential studied in section
10.2
.

Using the renormalized 1-electron-loop contribution to the Fourier transform of the Coulomb potential correction in equation (
275
), and performing the inverse Fourier transform, we obtain the renormalized 1-electron-loop contribution to the Coulomb potential correction as

\displaystyle V_{\mathrm{e,1L,electron}}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle\!=\!\int\widetilde{V}_{\mathrm{e,1L,electron}}(\mathbf{p})e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{r}/\hbar}\frac{d^{3}p}{(2\pi\hbar)^{3}}

\displaystyle\!=\!\frac{\hbar^{2}e^{2}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{3}\varepsilon_{0}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\!\frac{e^{ip_{\mathrm{r}}r\cos\theta_{\mathrm{r}}/\hbar}}{(p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon)}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38092pt\times p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}\sin\theta_{\mathrm{r}}d\phi_{\mathrm{r}}d\theta_{\mathrm{r}}dp_{\mathrm{r}}

\displaystyle\!=\!\frac{\hbar^{2}e^{2}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{3}\varepsilon_{0}}\!\frac{2\pi\hbar}{r}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\Big|_{0}^{\pi}\frac{ip_{\mathrm{r}}e^{ip_{\mathrm{r}}r\cos\theta_{\mathrm{r}}/\hbar}}{p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})dp_{\mathrm{r}}

\displaystyle\!=\!\frac{e^{2}}{4\pi^{2}\varepsilon_{0}r}\!\!\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\frac{ip_{\mathrm{r}}(e^{-ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar}\!-\!e^{ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar})}{p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})dp_{\mathrm{r}}

\displaystyle\!=\!\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi^{2}\varepsilon_{0}r}\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\!\!\frac{ip_{\mathrm{r}}(e^{-ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar}\!\!-\!e^{ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar})}{p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})dp_{\mathrm{r}}.

(277)

In the second equality of equation (
277
), we have expressed the momentum-space integral in spherical coordinates
(p_{\mathrm{r}},\theta_{\mathrm{r}},\phi_{\mathrm{r}})
. In the third and fourth equalities, we have carried out the integration with respect to the angular variables
\theta_{\mathrm{r}}
and
\phi_{\mathrm{r}}
. In the last equality of equation (
277
), we have applied the symmetry of the integrand with respect to
p_{\mathrm{r}}
to change the lower bound of the integral to the negative infinity.

As a complex function, the imaginary part of
\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})
in the integrand of equation (
277
) has branch cuts at the positive and negative imaginary axes starting at
p_{\mathrm{r}}=\pm 2im_{\mathrm{e}}c
and extending to infinities. This must be accounted for when applying the residue theorem to the integral of equation (
277
). Writing
p_{\mathrm{r}}=\pm\epsilon+ip_{\mathrm{ri}}
, where
p_{\mathrm{ri}}=\mathrm{Im}(p_{\mathrm{r}})
, we obtain

\mathrm{Im}[\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})]=\pm\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3}\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p_{\mathrm{ri}}^{2}}}\Big(1+\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p_{\mathrm{ri}}^{2}}\Big).

(278)

The positive and negative signs of equation (
278
) correspond to
\mathrm{Re}(p_{\mathrm{r}})>0
and
\mathrm{Re}(p_{\mathrm{r}})<0
, respectively.

Applying the residue theorem and the branch cuts of the integrand to calculate the integral of equation (
277
), we obtain

\displaystyle V_{\mathrm{e,1L,electron}}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle\!=\!\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi^{2}\varepsilon_{0}r}\Big\{\!\!-\!2\pi i\!\!\!\underset{p_{\mathrm{r}}=-\sqrt{i\epsilon}}{\mathrm{Res}}\Big[ip_{\mathrm{r}}\frac{e^{-ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar}}{p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt-\!2\pi i\!\!\underset{p_{\mathrm{r}}=\sqrt{i\epsilon}}{\mathrm{Res}}\Big[ip_{\mathrm{r}}\frac{e^{ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar}}{p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt-2\int_{\epsilon-i\infty}^{\epsilon-2im_{\mathrm{e}}c}\frac{ip_{\mathrm{r}}e^{-ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar}}{(p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon)}i\mathrm{Im}[\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})]dp_{\mathrm{r}}

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt+2\int_{-\epsilon+2im_{\mathrm{e}}c}^{-\epsilon+i\infty}\frac{ip_{\mathrm{r}}e^{ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar}}{(p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon)}i\mathrm{Im}[\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2})]dp_{\mathrm{r}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\!=\!\frac{e^{2}}{8\pi^{2}\varepsilon_{0}r}\Big\{-2\pi i\Big[\frac{1}{2}e^{ir\sqrt{i\epsilon}/\hbar}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-i\epsilon)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt-2\pi i\Big[\frac{1}{2}e^{ir\sqrt{i\epsilon}/\hbar}\Pi_{\mathrm{1L,electron}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}(-i\epsilon)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt+\!\frac{2\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3}\!\!\int_{\epsilon-i\infty}^{\epsilon-2im_{\mathrm{e}}c}\!\frac{p_{\mathrm{r}}e^{-ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar}}{(p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon)}\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p_{\mathrm{ri}}^{2}}}\Big(1\!+\!\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p_{\mathrm{ri}}^{2}}\Big)dp_{\mathrm{r}}

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt+\!\frac{2\alpha_{\mathrm{e}}}{3}\!\!\int_{-\epsilon+2im_{\mathrm{e}}c}^{-\epsilon+i\infty}\!\frac{p_{\mathrm{r}}e^{ip_{\mathrm{r}}r/\hbar}}{(p_{\mathrm{r}}^{2}-i\epsilon)}\sqrt{1\!-\!\frac{4m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p_{\mathrm{ri}}^{2}}}\Big(1\!+\!\frac{2m_{\mathrm{e}}^{2}c^{2}}{p_{\mathrm{ri}}^{2}}\Big)dp_{\mathrm{r}}\Big\}.

(279)

In the second equality of equation (
279
), we have calculated the residues and applied equation (
278
